92 
Southern Co (SO, Buy, TP USD99.50) 
Southern Co is an AIDC play that is part of our low-risk compounding theme and we rate the 
stock Buy on 2026-30e RAB CAGR of 10% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 8%) and 2026-30e EPS 
CAGR of 8% (2030-35e EPSCAGR of 10%) below the peer average as the company 
strengthens its balance sheet to 17% FFO/net debt by 2029. We forecast dividend growth of 3% 
to 2030e which is below EPS growth on a falling payout ratio in the range of 55-66%. We 
forecast adjusted TSR for FY26-32e of 12%; our as well as consensus EPS estimates are in 
line with company implied 2030 EPS guidance.  
Southern is an integrated utility operating in Georgia, Alabama and Mississippi. Georgia is the 
main growth driver with expected 2026-30 RAB CAGR of 14% versus single digit CAGRs for 
other regions and 10% for the company as a whole. The large load pipeline amounts to over 
75GW with 17GW contracted already and 8GW in advanced stages of discussion. The 
company expects to add 19GW of large load by 2030 and 25GW by 2035. 
The 2026-30 investment plan of USD81bn allocates cUSD68bn to regulated electric business, 
cUSD9bn to regulated gas business and cUSD4bn to other businesses. We forecast Southern’s 
CAPEX to rise to cUSD18.9bn per annum by 2030e from USD14.5bn in 2025 with most of the 
increase coming from Georgia.  
We consider Southern’s execution risk to be low for the following reasons: Nuclear delays and 
cost overruns are in the past and in line with other projects at the time; yet, it has given the 
company experience in large complex construction program. The balance sheet is improving 
and Southern operates in regions with excellent regulatory environments (Georgia and Alabama 
in Tier 1 and Mississippi in Tier 3). 
We believe the company has a resilient balance sheet with FFO/debt of 15.7% for 2026e.  
The company targets to reach FFO/debt of c17% by 2029 and our forecast is in line with the 
company’s target.  
In our view, catalysts for the stock include additional growth from data centres with gross 
pipeline of 75GW providing material upside to the investment plan leading to potential positive 
earnings revisions.  
We rate Southern Buy with a target price of USD99.50, implying 9.1% upside on DC growth in 
top Tier regulatory settings.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
96 
WEC (WEC, Hold, TP USD106.50) 
WEC is an AIDC play that is part of our low-risk compounding theme and we rate the stock Hold 
on 2026-30e RAB CAGR of 10% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 9%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 
8% (2030-35e EPSCAGR of 7%) below the peer average. We forecast dividend growth of 7% to 
2030e marginally behind EPS growth on a slightly falling payout ratio in the range of 66-68%. 
We forecast adjusted TSR for FY26-32e of 11%; our as well as consensus EPS estimates are 
at the top end of company implied 2030 EPS guidance.  
WEC is an integrated utility operating mainly in Wisconsin and Illinois. Wisconsin is the major 
growth driver and we model 2026-30 RAB CAGR of 11% versus 8% for Illinois. We model a 
46% increase in large load by 2030 assuming 3.9GW of new large load. 
The 2026-30 investment plan of USD37.5bn allocates cUSD29.1bn to electricity grids and 
generation and remaining to gas grids and LNG storage. The company plans to invest 
USD12.6bn in regulated renewables targeting capacity additions of 6.5GW during 2026-30.  
We forecast WEC’s capex to rise to cUSD7.4bn per annum by 2030e from USD3.7bn in 2025 
with most of the increase coming from Wisconsin.  
We consider WEC’s execution risk to be low for the following reasons: The company has an 
excellent track record of meeting or exceeding earnings guidance for 22 consecutive years and 
continues with its conservative approach and communication. WEC’s business mix shifts to 
Wisconsin, a Tier 1 regulatory environment in our framework, while Illinois, the other region, is 
Tier 4 in our framework with a history of contested cases and investment plan trimmings, yet 
some signs of improvements. 
We believe the company has a resilient balance sheet with FFO/debt of 16.6% for 2025 which is 
in line with its target of FFO/debt to be greater than 15%. We forecast FFO/net debt to reach 
c17% by 2030.  
In our view, catalysts for the stock include business plan updates and potential earnings revisions 
We rate WEC Hold with a target price of USD106.50, implying 2.0% downside as a well 
understood compounder with slightly below average growth.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
100 
Xcel (XEL, Buy, TP USD89.00) 
Xcel is an AIDC play as part of our high growth theme and we rate the stock Buy on 2026-30e 
RAB CAGR of 14% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 13%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 9% (2030-35e 
EPSCAGR of 10%) in line with the peer average. We forecast dividend growth of 6% to 2030e 
behind EPS growth on a falling payout ratio in the range of 53-58%. We forecast adjusted TSR 
for FY26-32e of 12%; our as well as consensus EPS estimates are in line with company implied 
2030 EPS guidance.  
Xcel is an integrated utility operating in Minnesota, Colorado and Southwestern regions. 
Minnesota and Southwestern regions are the primary growth drivers and we model 2026-30 
RAB CAGR of 13% and 27% respectively. The company has more than 20GW of large load 
pipeline with 2GW signed, expectations of further 1GW signed by yearend 2026 and target of 
further c3GW in 2027. We model a 22% increase in load by 2030 on DC and other drivers. 
Operating predominantly in states with high zero carbon power standards, 1GW of large load 
could equate to c10 GW of new renewables/storage capacity. 
The company notes USD13bn of additional investment opportunities on top of a 2026-30 
investment plan of USD60bn which allocates 49% to electricity grids, 39% to generation and 
remaining to gas grids and others. This base plan includes c11.4GW of renewables with c4GW 
of wind, c3.4GW of solar and c2.2GW of energy storage during 2026-30. We forecast Xcel’s 
CAPEX to rise to cUSD16.2bn per annum by 2030e from USD14.2bn in 2025 with most of the 
increase coming from Minnesota and Southwestern.  
We consider Xcel’s execution risk to be low for the following reasons: The company has an 
excellent track record of meeting or exceeding earnings guidance for 21 consecutive years and 
operates across a number of states with consistent energy transition targets. Regulatory risk is 
well diversified across differing markets coming in on average at mid Tier 3, in our view. 
We believe the company has a strong balance sheet with FFO/debt of 16.6% for 2025 which is 
in line with its target of FFO/debt to be greater than 15%. We forecast FFO/net debt to reach 
c17% by 2030.  
In our view, catalysts for the stock include additional growth from data centres with gross 
pipeline more than 20GW providing upside to the investment plan.  
We rate Xcel Buy with a target price of USD89.00, implying 16.6% upside on quality data centre 
and energy transition growth.

104 
AEP (AEP, Buy, TP USD145.90) 
AEP is an AIDC play as part of our high growth theme and we rate the stock Buy on 2026-30e 
RAB CAGR of 13% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 11%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 9% (2030-35e 
EPSCAGR of 9%) in line with the peer average. We forecast dividend growth of 9% to 2030e in 
line with EPS growth on a stable payout ratio of 55%. We forecast adjusted TSR for FY26-32e 
of 12%; our as well as consensus EPS estimates are at the top end of the company implied 
2030 EPS guidance.  
AEP operates pure wires as well as integrated utilities in multiple states like Ohio, Texas, 
Kentucky, Indiana, Virginia, etc including one of the largest transmission system in the US. The 
company highlights over USD10bn upside to the current 2026-30 investment plan of USD78bn 
which allocates cUSD50bn to electricity grids, cUSD8bn to renewables, cUSD13bn to new 
generation and remaining to maintenance of existing generation and other businesses. The 
company guides for c69GW of contracted load by 2030 with 45GW in ERCOT, 18GW in PJM 
and 6GW in SPP. We model a 68% increase in load by 2030 (with a haircut on the pipeline) and 
forecast AEP’s capex to rise to cUSD21.6bn per annum by 2030e from USD11.5bn in 2025 with 
most of the increase coming from grids.  
We consider AEP’s execution risk to be low for the following reasons: In absolute numbers, AEP 
has the largest pipeline with expectations of 69GW of contracted load by 2030. Yet, AEP 
highlights that its estimates are conservative and if demand should drop out, this would likely be 
back-filled easily from an overall pool of requests which is much larger. Regulatory risk is well 
diversified across differing markets. 
We believe AEP has a resilient balance sheet and we forecast FFP/debt of c14% in line with the 
company target FFO/debt range of 14-15%. 
In our view, catalysts for the stock include additional growth from data centres with the company 
seeing USD10bn of opportunities providing upside to the investment plan.  
We rate AEP Buy with a target price of USD145.90, implying 14.8% upside on wires-led data 
centre growth, conservative assumptions and diversified regulatory settings.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
108 
PPL (PPL, Buy, TP USD42.00) 
PPL is an AIDC play that is part of our low-risk compounding theme and we rate the stock Buy 
on 2026-30e RAB CAGR of 11% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 9%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 
8% (2030-35e EPS CAGR of 8%) just below the peer average. We forecast dividend growth of 
6% slightly below EPS growth on a falling payout ratio in the range of 60-54%. We expect 
adjusted TSR for FY26-32e of 12% and our and consensus EPS are at the top end of company 
implied 2029 EPS guidance.  
PPL is an electric wires as well as integrated electric utility operating in Kentucky, Pennsylvania 
and Rhode Island; it also has gas networks business. The current 2026-29 investment plan of 
USD23bn allocates cUSD15bn to electricity grids, cUSD6bn to generation and the remaining to 
gas and other businesses. We forecast PPL’s CAPEX to rise to cUSD7.8bn per annum by 
2030e from USD3.8bn in 2025 with an increase in investments across Kentucky, Invitium and 
Pennsylvania.  
We project a c58% increase in load growth for the company during 2026-30 (excl Invitium). PPL 
experiences strong data centre growth (11GW of signed electricity service agreements – ESAs 
and 21GW of letters of agreement - LOAs) in its wires business in Pennsylvania, which the 
company is well able to execute. We see upside to earnings guidance from data centre growth 
investments in the integrated business in Kentucky with 1.3GW of ESAs and 12.4GW of LOAs 
and from the Generation Company Joint Venture Invitium where the company has secured 
viable sites and inventory that supports 8-14GW of capacity.  
We consider PPL’s execution risk to be low for the following reasons: We believe the company 
can fully execute its growth plans with potential further growth that could start ramping in 
2029/30 from its joint venture. PPL operates in good regulatory environments (Tier 2/3 as per 
our analysis). 
We believe the company has a strong balance sheet and is on track to maintain its target 
FFO/debt ratio of 16-18%.  
In our view, catalysts for the stock include potential for positive earnings revision from higher 
data centre opportunities in Kentucky and via Invitium.  
We rate PPL Buy with a target price of USD42.00, implying 19.4% upside on high data centre 
growth and Invitium optionality.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
112 
PSEG (PEG, Hold, TP USD72.50) 
PSEG is part of our wires with overhang theme and we rate the stock Hold on 2026-30e RAB 
CAGR of 7% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 5%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 7% (2030-35e EPS 
CAGR of 4%) below the peer average. We forecast dividend growth of 7% to 2030e in line with 
EPS growth on a stable payout ratio of 65 %. We forecast adjusted TSR for FY26-32e of 10%; 
our as well as consensus EPS estimates are in line with company implied 2030 EPS guidance.  
PSEG is a regulated electricity and gas networks operator in New Jersey and Long Island with 
merchant nuclear generation assets. The 2026-30 investment plan of cUSD24-28bn allocates 
cUSD22.5-24.5bn to regulated electricity and gas networks grid and the remaining to nuclear 
maintenance and others. We forecast PSEG’s capex to rise to cUSD5.3bn per annum by 2030e 
from USD4.1bn in 2025 with the entire increase coming from networks business. We do not see 
data centre load growth as a significant part of PSEG’s investment case and believe political 
scrutiny is currently above average in New Jersey on affordability concerns. Having said that, 
there is potential upside from nuclear contracting similar to recent IPP deals and New Jersey’s 
state solar and storage targets could lead to related network investment opportunities. 
We consider PSEG’s execution risk to be high for the following reasons. We think the company 
can deliver against the investment plan and there could be upside. However, PSEG operates in 
New Jersey which we characterise Tier 5 in our regulatory framework on affordability concerns 
and rate freezes.  
We believe the company has a resilient balance sheet and is on track to achieve its targeted 
FFO/debt in the range of 14 -16% versus the downgrade threshold of 14%. 
Catalysts for the stock include political and regulatory decisions in New Jersey such as a 2027 
decisions on a rate case that the company could file in 4Q26 or updates around state 
generation investment plans. Nuclear contracting announcements could be a positive catalysts. 
We rate PSEG Hold with a target price of USD72.50, implying 1.4% upside as regulatory risks 
offset growth and nuclear contracting opportunities.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
116 
FirstEnergy (FE, Buy, TP USD50.00) 
FirstEnergy is an AIDC play and part of our Reform(ed)? theme and we rate the stock Buy on 
2026-30e RAB CAGR of 9% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 8%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 8% 
(2030-35e EPS CAGR of 8%) just below the peer average. We forecast dividend growth of 8% 
to 2030e in line with EPS growth on a stable payout ratio of 65%. We forecast adjusted TSR for 
FY26-32e of 12%; our as well as consensus EPS estimates are at the top end of the company 
implied 2030 EPS guidance.  
FirstEnergy operates a mix of electric and integrated utilities in Ohio, Pennsylvania, New Jersey, 
West Virginia, Maryland and New York and also has significant transmission business. We 
forecast a c27% increase in demand by 2030e (versus 2025) and believe that FirstEnergy has 
strong wires-led growth across its businesses with a 25GW data centre pipeline (9GW in Ohio, 
5GW in Pennsylvania, 5GW in Maryland, 4GW in West Viginia – Northern Virginia data centre 
alley adjacent – and 2GW in New Jersey). Beyond the 25GW identified pipeline, there is another 
c101GW of potential additions based on applications for detailed and conceptual load studies. 
The 2026-30 investment plan of USD36bn allocates cUSD19bn to transmission business,  
cUSD10bn to distribution and remaining to its integrated and other businesses. We forecast 
FirstEnergy’s capex to rise to cUSD6.9bn per annum by 2030e from USD4.9bn in 2025. The 
company expects 10% rate base CAGR for 2026-30e with rate base CAGRs of 13% for stand-
alone transmission, 10% for integrated and 10% distribution. 
We consider FirstEnergy’s execution risk to be medium for the following reasons: The company 
is coming to the end of a decade long repair of the balance sheet following the exit of the 
merchant business and Ohio governance crisis in 2020. FirstEnergy is diversified across a 
range of regulatory environments. 
We believe the company has rebuilt its balance sheet through asset sales and refocused the 
business on regulated activities with FFO/debt improving from 10% in 2020 to c13-14% by 
2026. S&P upgraded FirstEnergy’s rating to BBB+ (from BBB) in December 2025 and Moody’s 
put FirstEnergy on a positive outlook. We believe the company will achieve 14% FFO/net debt 
within two to three years. 
In our view, catalysts for the stock include positive earnings revisions on data centre and growth 
investments combined with potential for multiple expansion on improving balance sheet. 
We rate FirstEnergy Buy with a target price of USD50.00, implying 8.2% upside on data centre 
growth adjacent to data centre alley and balance sheet repair.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
120 
NiSource (NI, Buy, TP USD49.50) 
NiSource is an AIDC play as part of our high growth theme and we rate the stock Buy on 2026-
30e RAB CAGR of 11% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 11%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 10% 
(2030-35e EPS CAGR of 10%) which is above peer average. We forecast dividend growth of 
10% to 2030e in line with EPS growth on a stable payout ratio of 60%. We forecast adjusted 
TSR for FY26-32e of 14%; our estimate is above the top end of company implied 2030 EPS 
guidance while consensus is at the top end of the guidance.  
NiSource operates an integrated electric and gas utility in Indiana and gas LDCs in Ohio, 
Kentucky, Maryland, Pennsylvania and Virginia. In addition, NiSource has established a 
generation business – GenCo  – which helps with execution and financing of its large load 
growth ambitions of Norther Indiana Electric. The company has c9GW of large load pipeline with 
c4GW already signed, 3GW in advanced negotiations and another 2GW at an early stage.  
We model a 4GW increase in large load by 2030 implying 84% growth.  
The 2026-30 investment plan of USD28.6bn allocates c35% to NIPSCO, c40% to generation 
and remaining to GenCo. We forecast NiSource’s capex to rise to cUSD6.3bn per annum by 
2030e from USD3.3bn in 2025. The company provides long-term guidance to 2035, expecting a 
group RAB CAGR of 9-11%. We see NIPSO’s electric side as the growth driver; having said 
that, we model attractive growth in RAB for gas on resilience and replacement investments. 
We consider NiSource’s execution risk to be low for the following reasons: Relative to its size, 
NiSource has one of largest DC growth pipelines, yet we believe their GenCo (Generation 
Company that develops tailor-made for DCs) construct enables fast and efficient execution. 
NiSource highlights that their communicated 9GW pipeline is calibrated to the load that the 
system could absorb; connection requests run much higher. The company operates in 
reasonably constructive regulatory environment which we rank in Tier 2 and 3 in our analysis. 
We believe the company has a resilient balance sheet targeting FFO/debt in the range of 14-
16% versus our forecast of c14-15% during 2026-30.  
In our view, catalysts for the stock include additional growth from data centres providing upside 
to the investment plan. We see potential for positive earnings revisions.  
We rate NiSource Buy with a target price of USD49.50, implying 16.0% upside on high data 
centre growth with a boost from generation company.

124 
PG&E (PCG, Buy, TP Buy15.5) 
PG&E is part of our Reform(ed)? theme and we rate the stock Buy on 2026-30e RAB CAGR of 
9% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 9%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 9% (2030-35e EPS CAGR of 
9%) in line with average. We forecast 2026-30e DPS CAGR of 29% ahead of EPS growth on 
rising payout ratios rising from 10% to 20%. We forecast adjusted TSR for FY26-32e of 11%; 
our as well as consensus EPS estimates are slightly below the lower end of the company 
implied 2030 EPS guidance.  
PG&E is an electric and gas utility operating in California. The 2026-30 investment plan of 
USD73bn with cUSD53bn under California CPUC regulation and cUSD20bn under FERC 
transmission. However, as previously announced, after California did not pass expected reforms 
to wildfire liability and funding on 31 August 2026, PG&E cut its 2027 investments by USD2bn 
and is reviewing its longer-term spending plan given financing uncertainty. We forecast PG&E’s 
capex to rise to cUSD17bn per annum by 2030e from cUS10.6bn in 2025. In terms of data 
centres, we forecast a c10% increase in large load by 2030 on a pipeline that stands at 13GW 
as of June 2026 (up from 5GW in March 2026). 
We consider PG&E’s risks to be high for the following reasons: PG&E operates in California 
which is characterised by inverse condemnation and wildfire risk. However, the company has 
undertaken an extensive operational overhaul and wildfire mitigation program in the past five 
years which should lower ignition risks. There remains large uncertainty on wildfire legislation, 
yet we are optimistic that legislation that failed to pass in August could pass before year end or 
in 2027 on political acknowledgment that reform is needed. 
We believe the company has an adequate balance sheet coming a long way from bankruptcy 
proceedings in 2019. The company is targeting FFO/debt in mid-teens and we forecast FFO/net 
debt of c14% by 2030. 
In our view, transformative catalysts for the stock lie in California wildfire legislation. The 
package was not passed on 31 Aug. Yet, believing there is political acknowledgement of its 
necessity, utilities hope the reforms will still be passed in 2026 under Governor Newsom’s 
administration or in 2027 under a new administration. In addition, we see further growth 
opportunities in data centres. 
We rate PG&E Buy with a target price of USD15.50, implying 18.4% upside on potential reforms to 
wildfire legislation and an extensive wildfire mitigation programme that should lower ignition risk.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
128 
Edison Int. (EIX, Hold, TP USD56.50) 
Edison Int is part of our Reform(ed)? theme and we rate the stock Hold on 2026-30e RAB 
CAGR of 7% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 7%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 6% (2030-35e 
EPSCAGR of 8%) below the peer average. We forecast dividend growth of 6% to 2030e in line 
with EPS growth on stable payout ratio of 55%. We forecast adjusted TSR for FY26-32e of 
13%; our as well as consensus EPS estimates are at the lower end of the company implied 
2030 EPS guidance.  
Edison International is an electric utility operating in California with predominantly grids and a 
small residual capacity of generation assets. The 2026-30 investment plan of USD38-41bn 
allocates more than c85% to electric distribution, c11% for transmission and the remaining to 
other businesses. Further, around 12% of total capex is planned to be spent on wildfire 
management. We forecast Edison’s capex to rise to cUSD9.1bn per annum by 2030e from 
cUSD5.9bn in 2025.  
We consider Edison Int’s risk to be high for the following reasons: Edison Int operates in 
California which is characterised by inverse condemnation and wildfire risk and in the Eaton Fire 
from January 2025, Edison Int’s equipment is assumed to be involved. Damages are still 
unclear, yet on some estimates (source: Reuters) damages could exceed California’s wildfire 
fund’s capacity of cUSD20-23bn. 
Moody’s has a negative outlook on the company because of headwinds surrounding Eaton fire 
liabilities. Assuming prudent operations is grated, i.e., without specific Eaton liabilities, we 
forecast FFO/net debt of c17% and in line with the company’s target range. 
Catalysts for Edison Int. include Eaton catalysts such as the prudent operations determination 
and progress on claims settlement including a first estimate from the company on total claims, 
possibly in 2027. Further catalysts include California wildfire legislation. The package was not 
passed on 31 August, yet, believing there is political acknowledgement of its necessity, utilities 
hope the reforms will still be passed in 2026 under Governor Newsom’s administration or in 
2027 under a new administration.  
We rate Edison Int Hold with a target price of USD56.50, implying 1.0% upside as liabilities of 
Eaton wildfire remain unclear.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
132 
Centerpoint (CNP, Buy, TP USD44.00) 
Centerpoint is an AIDC play that is part of our wires with overhang theme and we rate the stock 
Buy on 2026-30e RAB CAGR of 12% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 8%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR 
of 9% (2030-35e EPS CAGR of 9%) in line with the peer average. We forecast dividend growth 
of 6% to 2030e behind EPS growth on a falling payout ratio in the range of 44-49%. We forecast 
adjusted TSR for FY26-32e of 11%; our as well as consensus EPS estimates are at the top end 
of the company implied 2030 EPS guidance.  
Centerpoint is a wires and pipes utility operating in Texas, Indiana and Minnesota. Electricity 
networks drive growth with electricity RAB are expected to grow at an attractive CAGR of c15% 
as per our estimate during 2026-30 compared to c6% for gas networks. We forecast a c64% 
increase in load by 2030e (versus 2025) and the company highlights strong growth drivers 
across industries that already drove 7-9% growth pa before additional demand from data 
centres. Batch Zero eligible submission in Texas include 10GW baseload, 4GW studied load 
and 3GW incremental demand. Centerpoint has c10GW of spare hosting capacity in its Texan 
grid and can move quickly to connect the first 10GW of demand at a low cost of cUSD800m. 
Following capacity, expansions or new, have not yet been fully reflected in the business plan. 
The 2026-30 investment plan of USD34.2bn allocates cUSD23.5bn to electric networks and 
cUSD10.6bn to gas networks, with the company estimating 10-year capital investments of 
USD66.7bn (not fully reflecting yet data centre growth). We forecast Centerpoint’s capex to rise 
to cUSD7.4bn per annum by 2030e from USD5.2bn in 2025 with the entire increase coming 
from electricity networks. Centerpoint also notes that there is a strategic advantage in Texas 
with development of transmission taking four years on average versus seven years in the rest of 
the US.  
We consider Centerpoint’s execution risk to be low for the following reasons: Centerpoint 
operates in states that rank well in our analysis (Texas in Tier 2 and Indian/Minnesota in tier 3). 
We believe the company is on a path to improve its operational performance and that regulation 
in Texas will remain constructive despite current political scrutiny. 
We believe the company has a resilient balance sheet targeting FFO/Debt in the range of 14-
15% versus credit rating downgrade  threshold of 12-13%. We forecast FFO/net debt of c14.5% 
by 2030e. 
In our view, catalysts for the stock include potential strategic plan upgrades and positive EPS 
revisions from increased data centre demand.  
We rate Centerpoint Buy with a target price of USD44.00, implying 8.5% upside on strong wires-
led data centre growth and operational improvements.

136 
Sempra (SRE, Buy, TP USD97.50) 
Sempra is an AIDC play as part of our high growth theme, and we rate the stock Buy on 2026-
30e RAB CAGR of 12% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 8%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 10% (2030-
35e EPS CAGR of 7%) above the peer average. We forecast dividend growth of 4% to 2030e 
behind EPS growth on a falling payout ratio in the range of 40-55%. We forecast adjusted TSR 
for FY26-32e of 13%; our as well as consensus EPS estimates are near the top end of 
company implied 2030 EPS guidance.  
Sempra is a wires and pipes utility operating in California and Texas with a small LNG business. 
Texas (Oncor) is the primary growth driver with 2026-30 RAB CAGR of 18% and EPS CAGR of 
c17%. The company highlights in its 2Q26 presentation: “Oncor’s overall interconnection queue 
increases to 298GW. 44GW of customer requests are under ERCOT evaluation in Batch Zero, 
representing potential 140%+ increase over Oncor's 31 GW peak demand.” Out of 44GW Batch 
Zero submissions, c27GW is baseload and c17GW studied load. We model a 48% increase in 
load by 2030e and more than doubling by 2035e. 
The 2026-30 base capital plan of USD65bn includes cUSD48bn for Texas and remaining for 
California. There is material upside in Texas: the company notes “improved confidence” in 
USD10bn incremental capital opportunities from 765kv line investments after recent ERCOT 
endorsement with additional investment opportunities from the Batch Zero process.  We 
forecast Sempra’s capex to rise to cUSD14.0bn per annum by 2030e from USD9.7bn in 2025 
with most of the increase coming from Texas Oncor.  
We consider Sempra’s execution risk to be low for the following reasons: After an EPS reset in 
2025 on lower earnings in California and infrastructure earnings volatility, we believe the 
company will regain market trust. In our view, their Californian business is largely insulated from 
wildfire risk and that Texan regulation will remain constructive, despite current political scrutiny. 
The company is transitioning into a regulated utility through infrastructure sales. 
We believe the company’s balance sheet is resilient achieving the target of FFO/debt at 100-
150bps above downgrade thresholds.  
In our view, catalysts for the stock include potential for positive earnings revisions and business 
plan upgrades.  
We rate Sempra Buy with a target price of USD97.50, implying 16.9% upside on strong wires-
led data centre growth.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
140 
Con Ed (ED, Hold, TP USD104.00) 
Consolidated Edison is part of our wires and overhang theme, and we rate the stock Hold on 2026-
30e RAB CAGR of 8% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 7%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 7% (2030-35e 
EPS CAGR of 6%) below the peer average. We forecast dividend growth of 6% to 2030e in line 
with of EPS growth on a stable payout ratio of 60% and expect adjusted TSR for FY26-32e of 10%. 
Our as well as consensus EPS estimates are in line with company implied 2030 EPS guidance.  
Consolidated Edison is a pure wire and pipes utility operating predominantly electricity (c70%) 
and gas (c30%) distribution networks in New York City, Westchester County and northern New 
Jersey and the company forecasts continued growth in winter peak electricity demand, with 
winter peak expected to exceed summer peak in the 2040s. Electrification of buildings and 
transportation are the keys drivers of electricity network investment, in addition to climate 
mitigation, while large load growth is not a material part of the growth outlook. Resilience and 
modernisation investments drive gas networks growth. The company expects 2025-29 RAB 
CAGR in CECONY of 7% versus 13% in O&R versus 7% in transmission. 
The recently published 2026-30 investment plan of USD37.7bn allocates cUSD37.1bn to energy 
delivery systems, cUSD0.5bn to Con Edison Transmission. We forecast Consolidated Edisons’s 
capex to rise to cUSD9bn per annum by 2030e with continued investment opportunities beyond. 
We see incremental upside to the investment plan coming from the reliability needs filing to 
meet the potential capacity shortfall in the 2030s, proactive planning, new transmission 
requirements to support decarbonisation ambitions and potential renewables development.  
We consider Consolidated Edisons’ risks being medium-to-low risk for the following reasons: 
We view the New York State Public Service Commission (PSC) as tough regulator (Tier 4) with 
a political overlay, yet we note some constructive improvements such as proactive approach to 
capex, enabling anticipatory spend and a weather normalisation mechanism. We believe the 
company is a conservative communicator, with a strong track record of delivering at the top-or-
above the guidance range. On the capex side, we view the plan as low risk, with a strong 
foundation of core capex and minimal reliance on large load growth. 
We believe the company has a much-improved balance sheet, and we forecast FFO/net debt of 
c18% in the next five years. We model equity issuance of USD3.9bn until 2030, that helps to 
keep leverage above threshold levels. We expect a dividend payout ratio of 60% over the next-
year period and note that Consolidated Edison has 52 consecutive years of dividend growth 
with a CAGR of 5.6%.  
In our view, we see minimal regulatory catalysts over the next 12 months. Rates for CECONY 
Electric and Gas are set until Dec 2028 following approval in January 2026 and together make 
up >90% of the rate base.  
We rate Consolidated Edison Hold with a target price of USD104.00, implying 7.4% downside 
on below average growth.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
144 
Eversource (ES, Hold, TP USD70.00) 
We see Eversource as a steady compounder, offering exposure to electrification infrastructure 
growth in the US North-East. From 2026-2030, we expect an RAB growth of 7% that is at the 
lower end of the regulated peer group. We view this asset growth as low-risk and driven primary 
by increased demand for electrification of residential and commercial demand, in addition to 
modernisation initiatives across the gas networks. Yet, the company lacks material upside from 
large load customers. This results in bottom line EPS 2026-30 growth CAGR of 6%, at the lower 
end of the peer group and we are broadly in line with consensus estimates. A much-improved 
balance sheet provides increased optionality for incremental transmission opportunities. We 
expect an average 2026-30 dividend payout ratio of 67%, resulting in 2026e dividend yield of 5%. 
We view the USD26.5bn capex programme as highly visible and low risk, stepping up 
USD2.3bn compared to the previous five-year forecast. Investments are split: USD9bn 
electricity distribution, USD5.6bn FERC regulated transmission and USD5.4bn natural gas 
distribution. We expect incremental upside could come from transmission investments later in 
the plan given the region is short generation; development restrictions make cross state and 
long-distance wires more likely. We see limited upside from large load industrial customers 
given higher power prices, restrictive development policy and limited generation capacity. 
Overall, the company has identified USD1.7bn of incremental investment opportunities that 
could materialise during and beyond the current forecast period. 
Regulatory updates: Eversource operates across FERC-regulated transmissions, Connecticut, 
Massachusetts and New Hampshire, that together equate to 35%, 28%, 29% and 7% of total 
rate base. We view the Connecticut regulator (PURA) as one of the more challenging. We are 
therefore cautious regarding the outcome of the recent CP&L Rate case filing, with a decision 
due mid-2027. Overall, we view FERC as a constructive regulator with attractive ROEs, 
however FERC’s ISO-NE Transmission ROE decision in March 2026 has resulted in an ROE 
reduction to 9.57% (vs 11.14% previously) and has ordered refunds from 2011 to 2026. 
Balance sheet: Concerns about the balance sheet are mostly in the past, in our view. Proceeds 
from the Aquarian Water sale (net USD1.7bn, closed in June 2026) and upcoming cash inflow from 
the Securitisation of storm costs (USD1.8bn) in 2027/28 provides flexibility. We note that Moody’s 
upgraded Eversource’s outlook from negative to stable in July. However, we expect the FERC 
refund payment (USD880mn) in 2026 and 2027 to put some short-term pressure on leverage. 
Risks and Catalyst: The Revolution Wind offshore wind project is mostly derisked. The next major 
event is likely to be the D.C. Circuit Court response to the Review and Motion for Stay regarding the 
FERC refund. On the regulatory side, the outcome of the CL&P Rate case is the next milestone. 
Valuation: We rate Eversource Hold with a target price of USD70.00, implying 4.7% upside due 
to the below average EPS growth profile and regulatory headwinds.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
148 
Exelon (EXC, Hold, TP USD44.00) 
Exelon is an AIDC play that is part of our wires with overhang theme and we rate the stock Hold 
on 2026-30e RAB CAGR of 8% (2030-35e RAB CAGR of 6%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 6% 
(2030-35e EPSCAGR of 4%) below peer average. We forecast dividend growth of 5% to 2030e 
behind EPS growth on a falling payout ratio in the range of 44-49%. We forecast adjusted TSR 
for FY26-32e of 10%; our as well as consensus EPS estimates are at the top end of the 
company implied 2029 EPS guidance.  
Exelon is a pure play regulated electricity and gas network utility operating in multiple states - 
Illinois, Pennsylvania, Maryland, Delaware, New Jersey and Washington DC.  We forecast a 
c16% increase in large load by 2030e (versus 2025) and c53% increase in 2035e. Transmission 
(20% of business in 2025) displays the highest growth with the company expecting a 2025-29 
CAGR of 16%. 
The 2026-29 investment plan of USD41.7bn focusses mainly on electricity networks with 
cUSD21.8bn for electric distribution, cUSD16.3bn for electric transmission and cUSD3.6bn for 
gas networks. We forecast Exelon’s capex to rise to cUS11.1bn per annum by 2029e from 
USD8.3bn in 2025 with the increase mostly coming from electricity networks.  
We consider Exelon’s execution risk to be medium for the following reasons: We see potential 
for 50%+ demand growth by 2035e yet believe this is offset by less constructive regulatory 
environments. The company operates in multiple jurisdictions which we rank Tier 3-5 in our 
analysis. For example, we see New Jersey experiencing political scrutiny and affordability 
concerns and Illinois having contested cases (albeit with signs of improvements). 
We believe the company has a resilient balance sheet targeting FFO/Debt of c14% versus 
downgrade threshold of 12-13%. We forecast FFO/net debt of c13.7% by 2030. 
In our view, business plan updates could be potential catalysts.  
We rate Exelon Hold with a target price of USD44.00, implying 1.1% upside on regulatory 
overhang which offsets growth opportunities.

Constellation (CEG, Buy, TP USD335.00) 
Constellation is part of our generator theme and we rate the stock Buy on 2026-30e EBITDA 
CAGR of 11% (2030-35e EBITDA CAGR of 1%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 19% (2030-35e 
EPS CAGR of 4%) above peer average. We forecast USD14bn of share buybacks in the next 
five years. We forecast adjusted TSR for FY26-32e of 15%; our as well as consensus EPS 
estimates are above the top end of the company implied 2029 EPS guidance . 
Constellation is an independent power producer operating in multiple regions and has recently 
expanded its business via the acquisition of Calpine. It has the largest fleets of nuclear, natural 
gas and geothermal generation in the US. We estimate two-third of earnings coming from PJM 
and nuclear fleet. We forecast Constellation’s capex to rise to cUS4.6bn per annum by 2030e 
from USD2.5bn in 2025 on upgrades and new builds. 
Between nuclear tax credits, which provide an inflation adjusted floor at c45USD/MWh (and 
rising), the explosion of capacity payments in PJM (which provide an uplift of c14-15USD/MWh 
to market prices), rising PJM forward prices of 60+USD/MWh and now long-term PPAs, the 
profitability of US nuclear has dramatically increased and been derisked. Assuming the nuclear 
fleet will be c75% contracted by 2032e, we model Constellation’s implied price for nuclear 
(across all categories) to rise from mid-forties in 2022-24 to mid-eighties in 2029/30e, while 
being materially derisked with a lower share of market price exposure. Including recent 
hyperscalers PPAs, circa one third of Constellation’s nuclear fleet is now supported by 
corporate PPAs. We assume that the company will continue to sign deals. 
We believe the company will use its cash for a combination of share buybacks (we model 
USD14bn during 2026-30e), selective acquisitions and organic growth. Constellation has a 
pipeline of c5GW in PJM of nuclear uprates and new capacity. 
In our view, Constellations risks are medium. Positive catalysts for the stock include further 
corporate PPAs, rising power prices or continued high capacity prices or extension of nuclear 
tax credits beyond 2032. Risks include pricing or PPA headwinds on affordability concerns. 
We rate Constellation Energy Buy with a target price of USD335.00, implying 23.4% upside on 
nuclear contracting upside.

Energy Transition ● Equities

156 
NRG Energy (NRG, Buy, TP USD179.00) 
NRG is part of our generator theme and we rate the stock Buy on 2026-30e EBITDA CAGR of 
6% (2030-35e EBITDA CAGR of -1%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 19% (2030-35e EPS CAGR 
of 6%), above the peer average. We forecast USD6.3bn of total share buybacks from 2027-30e. 
We forecast adjusted TSR for FY26-32e of 18% (FY26-32e); our 2030 EBITDA estimates are in 
line with consensus.  
NRG is an independent power producer operating in East, Texas and West and has recently 
expanded its business via the acquisition of a premier power portfolio from LS Power, which 
includes 18 natural-gas-fired facilities, adding c13 GW. We view the acquisition favourably as it 
increases geographic diversification, as well as exposure to the attractive structural trends in 
PJM, while fully matching NRG’s Texas retail portfolio. NRG’s retail footprint is primarily in 
Texas, where it is the largest supplier.  
We expect pricing momentum to strengthen from 2028 onwards, as new load growth starts to 
materialise in Texas and PJM, while a strong hedging profile provides downside protection 
against softer 2027 prices. We see spark spread expansion from 2027 to 2030. Higher capacity 
payments are secured until 2028, and we believe the PJM capacity market will remain tight, 
regardless of ongoing regulatory uncertainty. We view NRG as well placed to serve new-build 
data centre load growth, through the 5.4GW of development capacity for which it has secured 
equipment and labour. While progress on deploying this capacity has been slower than 
anticipated, momentum has improved of late with the company announcing a 1.2GW framework 
agreement in August 2026 with an AI hyperscaler that is expected to deliver USD500m of adj. 
EBITDA pa. While the deal is yet to be finalised, most of the commerical terms, but not the 
name of the counterparty, were announced with the 2Q 2026 results,indicating it is in advanced 
stages. We believe this deal could provide a scalable blueprint to deploy the remaining 4.2GW 
of capacity. We model a total of 2.4GW of gas-fired PPAs, starting in 2029 and 2030.  
We believe the company will use its cash for share buybacks (we model USD6.3bn from 2027-
30e), further selective acquisitions and selective organic growth. We expect NRG to meet its 3.0x 
net debt/EBITDA target by 2028. We think capital for new-build CCGTs contracted to data 
centres will make up the bulk of capex until 2030, followed by new gas uprates in PJM in the 
early 2030s. We note upside to this growth plan if more CCGTs PPAs come to fruition. Given the 
scale of the potential capex plan for new builds, management has stated it is open to bringing in 
equity partners to elevate balance sheet strain and/or enable continued share buybacks.  
In our view, catalysts for the stock include the evolution of forward power prices in Texas, 
ongoing regulatory developments in PJM and execution of the 5.4GW CCGT development 
pipeline. An extended pause to data centre developments in Texas could delay PPAs being 
signed, in addition to depressing forward prices. 
We rate NRG Buy with a target price of USD179.00, implying 72.5% upside; we like the 
potential pricing upside and strong cashflow generation.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
160 
Talen Energy (TLN, Buy, TP USD444.30) 
Talen is part of our generator theme and we rate the stock Buy on 2026-30e EBITDA CAGR of 
10% (2030-35e EBITDA CAGR of -7%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 25% (2030-35e 
EPSCAGR of 0%), above the peer average. We forecast USD5.5bn of total share buybacks in 
the next five years. We forecast adjusted TSR of 16% (FY26-32e) our EPS and EBITDA 
estimates are broadly in line with consensus estimates. 
Talen is an independent power producer with operations in Pennsylvania, Ohio and Indiana, 
mainly within the PJM market. The company recently expanded through the acquisition of 
Waterford Energy Center, Darby Generating Station and Lawrenceburg Power Plant from 
Energy Capital Partners, adding c2.6 GW of natural gas-fired generation capacity. Additionally, 
Talen acquired Freedom Generating Station and Guernsey Power Station, contributing a further 
c2.8 GW of natural gas-fired capacity. 
We model a tightening market from 2028, resulting in rising market prices and higher capacity 
prices (2026-30e), which increase by 90% in PJM. Talen secured a 17-year PPA for the bulk of 
the capacity at its Susquehanna power plant that runs until 2042, reducing market risk and 
reliance on nuclear PTCs. Given 85% of the nuclear fleet is contracted, we see limited scope for 
additional clean PPAs. For new build CCGTs, we believe the economics in PJM will remain 
challenging, curbing the upside for incremental data centre PPAs. However, we model 1.5GW 
of uprates for Talen’s gas fleet by 2032 and an extension to the coal assets. We see upside risk 
on pricing. This is most evident from 2028 onwards across Talen’s portfolio in PJM, given strong 
load growth and widening PPL basis spreads. We believe Talen is well placed in a scenario 
where generation capacity build-out is delayed, benefiting from market tightness and expanding 
spark spreads.  
We believe the company will use its cash for share buybacks (we model USD5.5bn until 2026-
30e), further selective acquisitions and selective organic growth. We think believe the balance 
sheet has ample headroom for either additional buybacks or uprates across the portfolio. We 
expect net debt/EBITDA to trend down to 1.4x by 2030e. Talen does not pay out any dividends 
and we expect all shareholder returns to come through share buybacks. 
In our view, catalysts for the stock include evolution of pricing in PJM and the outcomes of 
regulatory debates. M&A could also be catalyst with Talen better placed than the larger IPPs to 
compete for smaller portfolios and bolt on assets.   
We rate Talen Buy with a target price of USD444.30, implying 25.2% upside; we like the 
potential pricing upside and strong cashflow generation.

Energy Transition ● Equities
October 2026
164 
Vistra (VST, Buy, TP USD190.0) 
Vistra is part of our generator theme and we rate the stock Buy on 2026-30e EBITDA CAGR of 
9% (2030-35e EBITDA CAGR of -2%) and 2026-30e EPS CAGR of 21% (2030-35e EPS CAGR 
of 3%), above the peer average. We forecast USD14bn of total share buybacks in the next five 
years. We forecast adjusted TSR of 15% (FY26-32e); our 2030 EBITDA estimates are slightly 
ahead of consensus.   
Vistra is an independent power producer operating predominantly in PJM and Texas, but also 
has operations in the North-East and West Coast, alongside extensive retail operations. The 
company recently expanded its business via the acquisition of Cogentrix Energy from Quantum 
Capital Group, which includes 10 modern natural gas generation facilities totalling c5.5 GW of 
capacity across PJM, ISO New England, and ERCOT, with the transaction expected to close by 
YE 2026. Additionally, Vistra acquired seven natural gas generation facilities totalling c2.6 GW 
from Lotus Infrastructure Partners. Vistra’s retail portfolio’s primary focus is on Texas where it is 
the second largest supplier.  
We expect pricing momentum to strengthen from 2028 onwards, as new load growth starts to 
materialise in Texas and PJM, while a strong hedging profile provides downside protection 
against softer 2027 prices. We see spark spread expansion from 2027 to 2030. Higher capacity 
payments are secured until 2028, and we believe the PJM capacity market will remain tight, 
regardless of ongoing regulatory uncertainty. Vistra has signed two long-term nuclear PPAs with 
AWS and Meta, respectively, increasing the portion of contracted earnings and derisking the 
business. Vistra has the second largest nuclear fleet in the US and we see potential for 
additional PPAs given 50%/65% of the Texas and PJM fleet is contracted – further uprates are 
also an option. We believe any additional nuclear PPAs would be positive for the equity story.  
We believe the company will use its cash for share buybacks (we model USD14bn during 2026-
30e), further selective acquisitions and selective organic growth. We think Vistra has strong 
M&A capability and a good track record, but we see limited opportunities for further material 
transactions. In terms of new build capacity, we believe the Helix Digital Infrastructure platform 
offers an attractive opportunity to source new data centre PPAs. Vistra is the preferred power 
provider to the KKR, NVIDIA and KIA backed consortium.  
In our view, catalysts for the stock include the evolution of forward power prices in Texas, 
ongoing regulatory developments in PJM and progress developing the Helix pipeline. Further 
nuclear PPAs would also be a catalyst. In Texas, an extended pause to data centre 
developments could delay PPAs being signed and continue to soften forward prices.  
We rate Vistra Buy with a target price of USD190.00, implying 25.0% upside; we like the pricing 
and contracting upside with strong cashflow generation.
